2025-06-17T01:50:43.8511118Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8511289Z <pr_title>
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8511849Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8512544Z </pr_title>
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8512730Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8512914Z <pr_description>
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8514219Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8515043Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8515130Z ## Problem
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8515473Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8515933Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8516228Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8516526Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8516825Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8517124Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8517375Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8517620Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8518634Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8519453Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8519543Z ## Solution
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8519674Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8519780Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8520345Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8520803Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8521192Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8522004Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8522653Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8522951Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8523061Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8523360Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8523550Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8523647Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8523877Z ```bash
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8524383Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8524944Z ```
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8525050Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8525149Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8525382Z ```bash
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8526011Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8526734Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8526968Z ```
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8527064Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8527220Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8527468Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8527564Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8528144Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8528670Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8528748Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8529008Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8529496Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8530019Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8530624Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8531187Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8531504Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8531874Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8532352Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8532429Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8532544Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8532548Z

2025-06-17T01:50:43.8690809Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8691660Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8691855Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8692196Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8692342Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8693025Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8693578Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8694057Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8694613Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8694966Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8695452Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8695887Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8695974Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8696258Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8696610Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8696923Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8697129Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8697230Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8698214Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8699128Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8699209Z ### 4. Verification
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8699899Z After setup, the workflow will automatically connect to your Tailscale network using the `tag:ci` tag, allowing secure access to your OT-2 simulator for testing Prefect installations.
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8700804Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8701188Z Let me know once you've completed the OAuth client setup and added the GitHub secrets, and I'll activate the Tailscale integration.

2025-06-17T01:50:43.8706115Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8706394Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8706765Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8706858Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8707002Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8707223Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8707543Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8707618Z ```json
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8707794Z {
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8707971Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8708190Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8708458Z   },
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8708631Z   "acls": [
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8708811Z     {
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8709004Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8709235Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8709453Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8709655Z     }
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8709825Z   ]
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8709992Z }
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8710279Z ```
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8710378Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8710469Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8710610Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8710896Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8711401Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8711906Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8712376Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8712522Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8712621Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8712781Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8713246Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8713793Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8714012Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8714477Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8714914Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8715208Z 
2025-06-17T01:50:43.8715730Z The Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub Actions runner when it connects to your tailnet.

2025-06-17T01:57:57.7306962Z ## Problem
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7307484Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7308190Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7308662Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7309118Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7309532Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7309945Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7310523Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7310708Z 
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7311409Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7312310Z 
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7312441Z ## Solution
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7312628Z 
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7312776Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7313277Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7313692Z 
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7314345Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7315754Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7319238Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7319789Z 
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7319970Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7320676Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7321023Z 
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7321486Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7321901Z ```bash
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7322814Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7323822Z ```
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7323989Z 
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7324146Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7324794Z ```bash
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7325950Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7327293Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7327697Z ```
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7327869Z 
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7328113Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7328547Z 
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7328709Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-17T01:57:57.7329724Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
